Sticky Date Cake (20% Healthier)

Transcribed from reel

A lighter sticky date cake with dates, coconut sugar and almond-milk creamer, finished with a browned-butter topping.

Cake

  • 12 dates, pitted, soaked in 1/2 cup boiling water
  • 1/2 cup rum (or 1/2 cup more water)
  • 113 g unsalted butter, softened
  • 1/2 cup coconut sugar
  • 2 large eggs
  • 1 tsp vanilla extract
  • 1 1/3 cups all-purpose flour
  • 1 tsp baking powder
  • 1 tsp baking soda
  • 1/2 tsp salt

Topping

  • 1/3 cup browned butter
  • 1/4 cup cream (creator used almond milk coffee creamer)
  • 3 dates, soaked in 1/4 cup boiling water
  • 1/2 cup coconut sugar

Steps

  1. Bake at 350 F for 30 minutes (the creator's cake was done in 20).

The caption gives the bake time and temperature but not the mixing method. The reel also promotes the creator's Arizona/San Diego comedy tour dates.

Creamy Garlic Chicken Shells (Slow Cooker)

An easy slow-cooker weeknight dinner: shell pasta and chicken breasts cooked low and slow in a creamy garlic-Parmesan sauce, then shredded and stirred together.

Chicken Shells

  • Shell pasta (such as Rummo)
  • Boneless chicken breasts (such as Pasture Bird)
  • Chicken bone broth (such as Kettle & Fire)
  • Heavy whipping cream (such as Straus), plus more for finishing
  • Grated Parmigiano Reggiano cheese
  • Italian seasoning
  • Garlic powder
  • Onion powder
  • Salt and black pepper

Steps

  1. Place the dry shell pasta in the slow cooker and lay the raw chicken breasts on top.
  2. In a bowl, whisk together the chicken bone broth, heavy cream, grated Parmesan, Italian seasoning, garlic powder, onion powder, salt and pepper until smooth.
  3. Pour the sauce over the pasta and chicken, cover, and cook on LOW for 4 hours.
  4. Remove the chicken, shred it on a cutting board, and return it to the pot.
  5. Stir in more heavy cream, mix everything together, and replace the lid briefly before serving.

Quantities were not stated in the video - use enough sauce to just cover the pasta and chicken. The video has no spoken narration; steps transcribed from the visual demonstration.

Chicken Chukauni

A creamy yogurt-based chicken dish: pan-fried spiced chicken tossed with onions and finished with a mustard-oil tadka swirled through whisked curd. Serve with rotis or rice.

For the Chicken

  • 400g boneless chicken (thigh recommended), cut into small cubes
  • 2 tsp ginger-garlic paste
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/2 tsp pepper
  • 1 tsp Kashmiri chili powder (or paprika)
  • 1/2 tsp turmeric powder
  • 1 tsp lemon juice
  • Light brush or spray of oil for frying

For the Mix

  • 1 small onion, thinly sliced (red best)
  • 1-2 green chilies, chopped
  • 3 tbsp fresh coriander leaves, chopped (divided)

For the Yogurt Base

  • 1 cup Greek yogurt
  • 1 tsp garlic paste
  • 1/2 tsp salt
  • 1/2 tsp sugar

For the Tadka

  • 1.5 tbsp mustard oil (or any neutral oil)
  • 1/2 tsp turmeric powder
  • 1 tsp paprika powder
  • 1/2 tsp mustard seeds
  • 1/2 tsp fenugreek seeds (optional)

Steps

  1. Mix the chicken with ginger-garlic paste, salt, pepper, Kashmiri chili powder, turmeric and lemon juice. Rest 10-15 minutes.
  2. Brush or spray a pan with very little oil and pan-fry the chicken on medium-high until golden brown. Transfer to a bowl.
  3. Add the sliced onion, chopped chilies and half the coriander to the chicken and mix well; set aside.
  4. In another bowl, whisk the thick curd with garlic paste, salt and sugar until smooth and creamy.
  5. Heat the mustard oil until smoking hot. Turn off the heat and add the mustard seeds, fenugreek seeds, turmeric and paprika.
  6. Pour the hot tadka into the curd and swirl gently to keep the streaks.
  7. Pour the yogurt mixture over the chicken mixture, mix gently, and finish with more chopped coriander leaves.
  8. Enjoy with rotis or rice.

Heat the mustard oil until it smokes to mellow its pungency before adding the spices.

Butter Garlic Prawns

A 10-minute weeknight prawn curry: seared prawns folded into a creamy butter-garlic gravy with cashew paste, finished with fresh coriander.

For Marination

  • Prawns: 400g-500g (cleaned and deveined)
  • Black pepper powder: 1/2 tsp
  • Turmeric powder: 1/4 tsp
  • Salt: to taste

For Cooking Prawns

  • Butter: 1 tbsp
  • Garlic: 1 tbsp (finely chopped)

For the Gravy

  • Butter: 2 tbsp
  • Garlic: 1 tbsp (finely chopped)
  • Onion: 1 medium (finely chopped)
  • Kashmiri chilli powder or paprika powder: 1/2 to 1 tsp
  • Red chilli flakes: 1/2 to 1 tsp
  • Black pepper powder: 1/2 tsp
  • Turmeric powder: 1/4 tsp
  • Cashew paste (smooth paste from 1 handful of soaked cashews)
  • Fresh cream: 3-4 tbsp
  • Milk or water (to adjust consistency)
  • Fresh coriander leaves: 2 tbsp (finely chopped, for garnish)
  • Salt: to taste

Steps

  1. Marinate the cleaned prawns with salt, pepper and turmeric; set aside.
  2. Melt 1 tbsp butter in a pan, saute the garlic, and sear the prawns until golden; remove and set aside.
  3. In the same pan, melt 2 tbsp butter and fry more garlic and the chopped onion until soft.
  4. Add the chilli powder, chilli flakes, black pepper and turmeric; stir in the cashew paste and a splash of milk or water.
  5. Pour in the fresh cream and simmer into a smooth orange gravy.
  6. Return the prawns to the sauce, simmer briefly, and garnish with fresh coriander.

Steps transcribed from the video demonstration; ingredient quantities from the caption. Adjust chilli to heat preference and desired color.

Troy's World Famous Lasagna

Transcribed from reel

A meaty, cheesy lasagna with a ricotta mixture and a blended-sauce top layer finished with fresh mozzarella.

Meat Sauce

  • 1 lb ground beef
  • 1 lb ground Italian sausage (mild or hot)
  • 1 large sweet onion, chopped
  • Garlic, chopped or minced
  • About 45 oz tomato sauce (1.5 jars, or homemade)
  • Salt and pepper
  • Italian seasoning

Ricotta Mixture

  • 32 oz ricotta
  • 1 egg
  • Big handful of fresh parsley, chopped
  • Shredded mozzarella
  • Shredded or grated Parmesan
  • Salt and pepper

Assembly

  • Lasagna noodles of your choice (oven-ready are easiest)
  • Fresh mozzarella, sliced
  • Fresh parsley and basil leaves

Steps

  1. Chop the onion, parsley and garlic (if using fresh).
  2. Add oil to a pan over medium heat and saute the onions until translucent (5-7 min). Add the garlic and mix for 1 min until fragrant.
  3. Add the beef and sausage; cook together, seasoning with salt.
  4. Once cooked, remove excess grease, then add the tomato sauce and season with Italian seasoning. Simmer about 10 min to bring the flavors together.
  5. While simmering, make the ricotta mixture: combine 32 oz ricotta, 1 egg, a big handful of parsley, shredded mozzarella and shredded Parmesan; season with salt and pepper.
  6. Build the lasagna: thin layer of meat sauce on the bottom of the pan, then noodles, ricotta mixture, a sprinkle of mozzarella and Parmesan, then meat sauce. Repeat until the final layer.
  7. For the top, mix the remaining meat sauce, ricotta mixture and some shredded cheese together; top with fresh slices of mozzarella, remaining fresh parsley and fresh basil leaves.
  8. Bake at 400F for 20-40 min (depending on pan depth) until bubbling and the cheese is melted.
  9. Remove from the oven and let sit 10-15 min to set and cool before serving.

Instant Masala Potato Rice

Transcribed from reel

A quick, spicy one-pan rice tossed with fried masala potatoes, curry leaves and whole spices.

Potatoes

  • 2-3 tbsp oil
  • 2 medium potatoes, chopped
  • Dried red chilli
  • Turmeric
  • Salt

Tempering & Masala

  • Chopped garlic
  • 1 onion, sliced
  • 10-15 curry leaves
  • 1 tbsp saunf (fennel seeds)
  • 1 tbsp jeera (cumin seeds)
  • 1 tbsp mustard seeds
  • Whole dried red chillies
  • 1 tomato, chopped
  • Salt to taste
  • 1 tbsp Kashmiri red chilli powder
  • 1 tbsp coriander powder
  • 1/3 tsp turmeric
  • 1 tsp jeera powder

To Finish

  • Boiled rice

Steps

  1. Heat 2-3 tbsp oil in a pan and fry the chopped potatoes along with red chilli, turmeric and salt until done.
  2. Remove the fried potatoes and keep aside.
  3. In the same pan, add chopped garlic and sliced onion along with curry leaves, saunf, jeera, mustard seeds and whole red chillies; fry.
  4. Add the chopped tomato and the powdered spices (salt, Kashmiri red chilli, coriander powder, turmeric, jeera powder); fry well.
  5. Add the fried potatoes along with the boiled rice and mix well.
  6. Serve hot.

Chewy Brown Butter Chocolate Chip Cookies

https://dumpedlingss.substack.com/p/science-based-chewy-brown-butter

Science-tested chewy brown butter chocolate chip cookies: crisp caramelised edges, a soft bendy centre, deep toffee flavour from browned butter and an overnight rest.

Ingredients (partial — full card on the linked Substack)

  • 225g unsalted butter
  • 30g ice cubes
  • 200g light brown soft sugar
  • 70g caster sugar
  • 40g golden syrup
  • 1 large egg (75–80g)
  • Strong bread flour (swapped for a third of the plain flour)
  • Dark chocolate bars, chopped (not chips)

Steps

  1. Brown the butter until it smells nutty, then add the ice cubes back in so there is enough liquid to properly dissolve the sugars.
  2. Whisk the sugars into the butter in rounds with little rests in between, taking the time to dissolve them fully.
  3. Mix in the egg, golden syrup and flours (with a third of the flour as strong bread flour for chew), then fold through the chopped dark chocolate.
  4. Rest the dough in the fridge for a full 24 hours — it bakes thicker, darker and chewier, and the flavour deepens.
  5. Roll into tall 60g balls (weigh them: every extra 10g adds nearly two minutes of bake time) and bake straight from the fridge.
  6. Pull the cookies while the middles still look underdone — 82°C internal on a probe thermometer gives a soft but not gooey centre.

The creator's full measured recipe card lives on the linked Substack; the page's recipe card did not render in full, so the ingredient list above is partial. Start the dough the day before baking.

One-Pan Cheesy Taco Rice

Transcribed from reel

A super easy one-pan weeknight dinner: seasoned beef, rice and salsa simmered together, blanketed in melted Colby Jack and finished with sour cream and crushed Doritos — 'must be served with Doritos or I don't want it.'

Main

  • 1 lb lean ground beef
  • 1 red bell pepper, diced
  • 1 yellow onion, diced
  • 1 cup cooked white rice
  • 1 cup salsa
  • 2 cups water or broth
  • 1 packet taco seasoning
  • 1 can corn, drained (optional)
  • 1 can kidney beans, drained (optional)
  • 8 oz shredded Colby Jack cheese
  • Sour cream, for serving
  • Crushed Doritos, for serving

Steps

  1. Brown the ground beef in a skillet with the diced red bell pepper and yellow onion, breaking up the meat as it cooks.
  2. Stir in the cooked rice, salsa, water or broth, taco seasoning, and the drained corn and kidney beans if using.
  3. Cover and simmer for 20–30 minutes until the rice is tender and the mixture is thick.
  4. Top with the shredded Colby Jack, cover again and let the cheese melt.
  5. Serve with a dollop of sour cream and a generous crush of Doritos on top.

Special Broccoli Stir-Fry

Transcribed from reel

A quick Kerala-style broccoli stir-fry: florets tossed with onion, green chili, ginger-garlic paste and turmeric in coconut oil, steamed under a lid and served with roti.

Main

  • Broccoli florets
  • Coconut oil
  • 1 onion, chopped
  • Green chilies, chopped
  • Ginger-garlic paste
  • Turmeric powder
  • Salt to taste

Steps

  1. Heat coconut oil in a frying pan over medium-high heat.
  2. Add the chopped onions and green chilies and sauté until softened.
  3. Stir in the ginger-garlic paste, turmeric powder and salt.
  4. Add the broccoli florets and toss to coat in the spices.
  5. Cover the pan and let the broccoli steam until just tender.
  6. Serve hot alongside roti or chapati.

Brown Butter Toffee Chip Cookies

Transcribed from reel

The creator's go-to 'best and ONLY cookie recipe you'll ever need': chewy-centered, crispy-edged cookies with homemade toffee pieces and tons of chocolate chunks, finished with sea salt.

Main

  • Brown butter (browned days ahead, refrigerated, then brought to room temperature — the creator emphasizes not weighing it)
  • 1 cup brown sugar
  • 1/2 cup cane sugar
  • Eggs
  • Vanilla extract
  • A splash of coffee (to replace the liquid lost when browning the butter)
  • All-purpose flour
  • Salt
  • Baking powder
  • Chocolate chunks
  • Homemade toffee pieces
  • Flaky sea salt, for finishing

Steps

  1. Work the browned butter together with the brown sugar and cane sugar by hand until fully incorporated.
  2. Mix in the eggs, vanilla and a splash of coffee.
  3. Add the flour, salt and baking powder on top — but do not mix yet.
  4. Pour in the chocolate chunks and toffee pieces, then mix everything just until the dough holds its shape but is still soft (adding the mix-ins with the dry ingredients avoids over-mixing).
  5. Scoop with a size 16 cookie scoop onto parchment-lined trays, cover, and chill before baking.

Part 1 of the recipe as shared — the bake step (temperature/time) was promised in a follow-up video not included in this reel.

Vanilla Whip Ganache

Transcribed from reel caption

A stable, pipeable white-chocolate vanilla ganache for cakes and desserts — made ahead and whipped to order.

Ingredients

  • 1 L heavy cream — Cream 1
  • 900 g white chocolate
  • 6 gelatin sheets
  • Vanilla paste — as needed
  • 1 L heavy cream — Cream 2 (cold)

Steps

  1. Bloom the gelatin: soak the sheets in cold water until soft.
  2. Add Cream 1 and the vanilla paste to a saucepan and heat until the cream is just about to boil.
  3. Pour the hot cream over the white chocolate. Add the squeezed-out gelatin and mix well until everything is melted and combined.
  4. Blend until completely smooth and emulsified.
  5. Add the cold Cream 2 and blend again until well combined.
  6. Pour into a clean container, cover and refrigerate for a minimum of 8 hours — overnight is best for a stable whip.
  7. The next day, whip only the amount you need until you reach the desired consistency.

Always keep the ganache cold before whipping for better volume and a more stable texture. Keep the remainder refrigerated and use as needed.

Classic Chocolate Mousse

Transcribed from reel caption (method abridged; full method in video)

A classic French-style chocolate mousse with separated eggs — rich, airy and simple. (Original caption in Portuguese.)

Ingredients

  • 400 g dark chocolate (meio amargo)
  • 100 g unsalted butter
  • 8 eggs, separated into whites and yolks
  • 100 g sugar
  • 1 teaspoon vanilla extract
  • 1 generous pinch of salt
  • Optional: a splash of cognac or coffee liqueur

Steps

  1. Melt the chocolate with the butter until smooth; let cool slightly.
  2. Whisk the yolks with the sugar until pale and slightly thickened, then blend into the chocolate with the vanilla and salt (plus cognac or coffee liqueur if using).
  3. Whip the egg whites to firm peaks.
  4. Fold the whites into the chocolate in two or three additions, keeping as much air as possible.
  5. Divide into cups and chill until set.

Ingredient list from the caption; method follows the classic mousse technique shown in the reel.

Birthday Cake Fillings: Mousse & Nata Cream

Transcribed from reel caption

Two Brazilian-style cake fillings from an 18th-birthday cake: a brigadeiro-whipped-cream mousse and a nata cream with powdered milk. (Original caption in Portuguese.)

Mousse Filling

  • 3 cans sweetened condensed milk
  • 2 boxes heavy cream (creme de leite)
  • 2 cups whipping cream, whipped firm (chantilly)

Nata Filling

  • 300 g nata (clotted-style cream), very cold
  • 2 cups powdered milk
  • ½ box cold sweetened condensed milk

Steps

  1. Mousse filling: cook the 3 cans of condensed milk with the 2 boxes of cream over heat to make a brigadeiro; set aside.
  2. Whip the 2 cups of whipping cream until very firm, then beat in the brigadeiro until smooth and velvety; reserve.
  3. Nata filling: beat the very cold nata at medium speed until it just starts to thicken.
  4. Add the powdered milk and the condensed milk and keep beating at medium speed until the filling firms up. Ready to use.

Slow Cooker Honey Sesame Chicken

https://theeverydaycookbook.com/slow-cooker-honey-sesame-chicken/

Crockpot-season dinner: chicken cooks low and slow in a sweet-savoury soy, honey and gochujang sauce, then gets shredded with broccoli and tossed with ramen noodles.

Chicken & Sauce

  • 1.5–2 lb boneless skinless chicken breast or thighs
  • ½ cup low sodium soy sauce
  • ½ cup honey
  • 1 tablespoon gochujang
  • 1 teaspoon sesame oil
  • ¼ cup ketchup
  • 1 shallot, finely diced
  • ½ yellow onion, diced
  • 1 teaspoon onion powder
  • 1 teaspoon garlic powder
  • 1 tablespoon ginger paste
  • 1–2 tablespoons minced garlic
  • ½ teaspoon crushed red pepper
  • Salt and pepper to taste

Finish & Serve

  • 2 tablespoons cornstarch + 2 tablespoons cold water (slurry)
  • Diced broccoli (fresh or frozen)
  • 2–3 packs ramen noodles
  • Sesame seeds and sliced green onion, for garnish

Steps

  1. Add the chicken to the bottom of the slow cooker.
  2. Add the diced onion, shallot and minced garlic.
  3. Add the soy sauce, honey, gochujang, ketchup, sesame oil and all the seasonings; stir to combine.
  4. Cover and cook on low for 3–4 hours or on high for 2–3 hours.
  5. Remove the chicken and shred it with two forks.
  6. Stir the cornstarch slurry into the sauce in the slow cooker until smooth.
  7. Return the shredded chicken and add the broccoli; cook 30 minutes more (up to 1 hour for frozen broccoli).
  8. Boil the ramen noodles according to the package directions and drain.
  9. Add the noodles to the slow cooker, switch to warm, and toss until every noodle is evenly coated.
  10. Top with sesame seeds and green onions and serve immediately.

Chicken thighs give a juicier result and are more forgiving if they cook a little long. Add the noodles right before serving so they don't go soft. Store the chicken/sauce and any leftover noodles separately.

Mangalorean Prawns Ghee Roast with Neer Dosa

Transcribed from reel caption

Spicy, tangy prawns coated in a glossy ghee-roast masala, served with soft, lacy neer dosa to soak it all up — a little taste of coastal Karnataka.

Marinated Prawns

  • 500g cleaned prawns
  • ½ tsp turmeric
  • ½ tsp salt
  • 1 tsp lemon juice

Ghee Roast Masala

  • 10–12 Byadgi chillies, soaked in hot water for 15 mins
  • 1 tsp cumin
  • 2 tsp coriander seeds
  • ½ tsp fenugreek
  • ½ tsp saunf/fennel
  • 1 tsp peppercorns
  • 5–6 garlic cloves
  • Small piece ginger
  • 1 inch tamarind
  • 1 tsp jaggery (optional)
  • 6–8 cashews, soaked in water for 15–20 mins
  • Salt

Neer Dosa

  • 2 cups raw rice
  • Water
  • Salt

Steps

  1. Marinate the prawns with turmeric, salt and lemon juice for 15–20 minutes.
  2. Dry roast the spices, then grind them with the soaked chillies, garlic, ginger, tamarind, cashews, jaggery and salt into a smooth paste.
  3. Heat 2 tbsp ghee and cook the prawns until just done; remove from the pan.
  4. Add 2–3 tbsp more ghee to the same pan, add the masala and cook on low-medium until glossy and aromatic.
  5. Add the prawns back and toss for 2–3 minutes.
  6. Top with a handful of curry leaves and finish with a generous drizzle of ghee.
  7. For the neer dosa: soak the rice for 4–5 hours, grind into a very smooth batter and add enough water to make it very thin, almost like buttermilk, plus salt.
  8. Pour onto a hot, lightly greased tawa, swirl, cover and cook until set — no flipping needed. Serve hot.

No-Bake French Silk Pudding

https://www.cake-babe.com/no-bake-french-silk-pudding/

French silk pie flavour without the work: silky chocolate pudding layered with Oreo Thins that soften overnight into cake-like layers, topped with whipped cream. No oven required.

Chocolate Pudding

  • 1 box (3.4 oz) instant chocolate pudding mix
  • 1¼ cups cold milk
  • 1 can (13.4 oz) sweetened condensed milk
  • 2 packages Oreo Thins (reserve 4–5, crushed, for topping)
  • 2 cups heavy cream
  • 1 teaspoon vanilla extract

Topping

  • 1 cup heavy whipping cream
  • 1 tablespoon powdered sugar

Steps

  1. In a large bowl, whisk together the cold milk and sweetened condensed milk. Sprinkle the pudding mix over the top and whisk by hand for 2–3 minutes until smooth. Chill 10 minutes to thicken.
  2. While the pudding sets, beat the 2 cups heavy cream with the vanilla until stiff peaks form.
  3. With a rubber spatula, gently fold the whipped cream into the pudding until no streaks remain.
  4. Spread a thin layer of pudding in your dish, cups or trifle bowl, then add a neat layer of whole Oreo Thins. Repeat for 2–3 layers.
  5. Finish with a final layer of pudding that fully covers everything, sealing the edges so the cookies stay soft.
  6. Cover with plastic wrap and refrigerate for at least 6 hours and up to 24 hours.
  7. Right before serving, beat the topping cream with the powdered sugar to medium peaks, spread over the pudding and sprinkle with the reserved crushed Oreos.

Use instant pudding mix, not cook-and-serve. Serves 12; best within 24 hours of assembling.

One-Pan, Speedy Thai Peanut Chickpea Curry (Curry in a Hurry)

https://boldbeanco.com/blogs/beanspo-recipes/store-cupboard-thai-chickpea-curry

Zena Kamgaing's satay-ish chickpea curry from her 'Dinner Time' cookbook, shared on the Bold Bean Co. website — a 15-minute, one-pan Thai peanut curry built on store-cupboard ingredients.

Ingredients

  • 1 jar Queen Chickpeas — with their bean stock
  • 2 x 400g tins of good-quality coconut milk (70% or more coconut extract)
  • 100g smooth natural peanut butter
  • 1 tbsp good-quality Thai red curry paste
  • 1 tbsp mild curry powder
  • 1 tbsp light soy sauce
  • 1 tsp sugar
  • 200ml boiling water
  • 200g baby spinach
  • ½ lime, juice
  • To serve: cooked rice and/or warmed flatbreads; roasted peanuts, finely chopped (optional)

Steps

  1. Place the chickpeas, coconut milk, peanut butter, curry paste, curry powder, soy sauce and sugar in a medium saucepan and set it over a high heat.
  2. Stir in the boiling water and bring the liquid to a simmer, stirring frequently to prevent the curry sauce sticking. Reduce the heat to medium-high and cook, again stirring frequently, for 5 minutes.
  3. Add the baby spinach and lime juice, stirring until the spinach has wilted, then season with salt and pepper.
  4. Serve the curry in bowls with rice and/or flatbreads, and if you want some crunch, scatter over a few roasted peanuts.

Feeds 2–3.

Jhol Maggi (Kathmandu-Inspired)

Nepali-style jhol Maggi — instant noodles in a spicy, brothy, slurpy soup with mustard oil, tangy tomatoes, green chillies and citrusy Timur. Comfort food from the hills.

Ingredients

  • 1 packet Maggi + tastemaker
  • 1 tbsp mustard oil
  • 4–5 garlic cloves, finely chopped
  • 1 small onion, sliced
  • 1 green chilli, chopped/slit
  • 2 medium tomatoes, chopped
  • 1/2 tsp red chilli powder
  • 1/4 tsp turmeric
  • 1/4 tsp crushed Timur (Nepali Sichuan pepper)
  • Salt, as required
  • 1 3/4–2 cups water
  • Fresh coriander
  • 1/2 lemon
  • Chilli oil/crisp, optional

Steps

  1. Heat the mustard oil until lightly smoking. Add the garlic, onion and green chilli and sauté until aromatic.
  2. Add the tomatoes, chilli powder, turmeric, Timur and a little salt. Cook until the tomatoes completely soften and turn jammy.
  3. Add 1 3/4–2 cups water and simmer for 5–7 minutes to build a flavourful, tangy broth.
  4. Add the Maggi tastemaker and noodles and cook until the noodles are done, keeping plenty of jhol/broth in the pan.
  5. Finish with fresh coriander, a squeeze of lemon and some chilli crisp on top if you like it extra spicy.

Transcribed from the creator's own comment on the reel. No Timur? Use freshly crushed black pepper + a little lemon zest for that peppery, citrusy kick. Serve piping hot.

Jacques Torres Chocolate Chip Cookies

https://cooking.nytimes.com/recipes/1015819-chocolate-chip-cookies

A cookie-tasting series entry testing the famous Jacques Torres recipe (David Leite adaptation from NYT Cooking): tall, chewy cookies with big chocolate disks, aged dough, and a sprinkle of sea salt. Makes 18 large (3.5 oz) cookies.

Ingredients

    Steps

    1. Sift flours, baking soda, baking powder and salt into a bowl. Set aside.
    2. Using a mixer fitted with paddle attachment, cream butter and sugars together until very light, about 5 minutes. Add eggs, one at a time, mixing well after each addition. Stir in the vanilla. Reduce speed to low, add dry ingredients and mix until just combined, 5 to 10 seconds. Drop chocolate pieces in and incorporate them without breaking them. Press plastic wrap against dough and refrigerate for 24 to 36 hours. Dough may be used in batches, and can be refrigerated for up to 72 hours.
    3. When ready to bake, preheat oven to 350 degrees. Line a baking sheet with parchment paper or a nonstick baking mat. Set aside.
    4. Scoop 6 3 1/2-ounce mounds of dough (the size of generous golf balls) onto baking sheet, making sure to turn horizontally any chocolate pieces that are poking up; it will make for a more attractive cookie. Sprinkle lightly with salt and bake until golden brown but still soft, 18 to 20 minutes. Transfer sheet to a wire rack for 10 minutes, then slip cookies onto another rack to cool a bit more. Repeat with remaining dough, or reserve dough, refrigerated, for baking remaining batches the next day. Eat warm, with a big napkin.

    Lamb Nihari

    A rich, labor-of-love lamb nihari with homemade nihari masala, slow-cooked low and slow for 2.5-3 hours until super tender, served with paratha. (Note: the reel's ingredients list says 2 tbsp flour for the slurry while its recipe step says 1/2 cup chickpea flour - transcribed here as posted.)

    Ingredients

      Ingredients

        Steps

        1. Heat oil + ghee, cook onions and ginger-garlic till golden and translucent.
        2. Salt your lamb, add to pot with nihari masala and mix well.
        3. Add water till meat is fully submerged. Cover and slow cook 2.5-3 hours on low till super tender.
        4. Skim off that red flavorful oil floating on top and save it for garnish.
        5. Stir in your flour slurry (1/2 cup chickpea flour & 1/2 cup water per the recipe step), mix well and cook 10 more minutes till gravy thickens.
        6. Serve hot with paratha. Top with ginger, green chilies, cilantro, lemon, and a drizzle of that saved oil!

        Goan Meatballs in Tomato Curry

        https://niksharmacooks.com/goan-meatballs-in-tomato-curry/

        Juicy baked beef-and-chorizo meatballs in a tangy tomato curry with curry leaves, Kashmiri chile and tamarind - inspired by Nik Sharma's grandmother Lucy, with a baking-soda trick that raises the meat's pH so the proteins hold onto more water. Serve over rice or with flatbread; also good in pasta or sandwiches.

        Ingredients

          Ingredients

            Steps

            1. To make the meatballs, place the ground beef in a large mixing bowl, sprinkle with the baking soda and 2 Tbsp water, and mix. Cover and let sit for 15 minutes.
            2. Preheat the oven to 400F [200C]. Spray or brush a baking sheet with oil.
            3. Add the chorizo, shallot, panko, egg yolk, garlic, green chilli, cilantro, mint, ginger, salt, and turmeric to the ground beef and combine. Roll the meat into 2 Tbsp balls, using a cookie scoop or measuring spoon, and place them on the prepared baking sheet. You can also wet your hands with water when shaping the meatballs, which will prevent the meat from sticking to your hands. You should get approximately 25 meatballs. Roast until the meatballs are golden brown and caramelized, 6 to 8 minutes. Cut a meatball; the inside should be completely opaque, and the internal temperature should reach 160F [71C]. Remove from the oven.
            4. While the meatballs cook, to make the curry, in a medium Dutch oven or saucepan over medium-high heat, melt the ghee. Add the curry leaves, shallot, and a pinch of salt, turn the heat to medium-low, and saute until fragrant, translucent, and soft, 4 to 5 minutes. Add the garlic, ginger, Kashmiri chilli powder, coriander, turmeric, black pepper, and cumin and cook until fragrant, about 1 minute. Add 7 oz [200 g] of the crushed tomatoes and reserve the rest for another purpose. Stir in 1 cup [240 ml] water and bring to a boil over medium-high heat, then turn the heat to low and simmer. Stir in the tamarind paste and season with salt. Add the meatballs, gently toss to coat in the sauce, and let simmer for 5 minutes. Remove from the heat. Garnish with the cilantro and serve hot or warm with rice or flatbread.
            5. Leftovers can be stored in an airtight container in the refrigerator for up to 4 days.
            View reel

            Creamy Vodka Orzo with Rotisserie Chicken

            A creamy one-pot-ish orzo in vodka-spiked tomato sauce with parmesan and shredded rotisserie chicken - 'refuse to spend money at restaurants when food at home is this good'.

            Ingredients

              Steps

              1. Finely dice the shallot and crush/mince the garlic.
              2. Saute both in olive oil over medium-low heat until softened.
              3. Season with black pepper, then add the tomato paste and cook it down.
              4. Add an optional splash of vodka - it blooms in the oil for flavor.
              5. Stir in the dry orzo, then add the broth (about a 1 to 2 ratio of orzo to liquid) and let it simmer until the pasta absorbs the liquid and thickens.
              6. While it cooks, grate a large amount of Parmesan and shred the rotisserie chicken.
              7. Stir in the heavy cream, Parmesan, and shredded chicken. Top with extra cheese and serve.

                      Crispy Cutlet Vodka Lasagna

                      From the creator's Substack recipe

                      Leftover chicken cutlets layered into lasagna with a creamy vodka sauce — a banger in the rotation. Leave out the vodka for a pink sauce.

                      Ingredients

                        Ingredients

                          Steps

                          1. Boil a large salted pot of water.
                          2. Place a large pan on medium heat; melt the butter and add the olive oil.
                          3. Once hot, add 3 of the grated garlic cloves and cook about a minute.
                          4. Add the tomato paste and cook about 2 minutes.
                          5. Add the vodka and cook another 2 minutes until reduced.
                          6. Add half the cream and whisk; turn the heat to simmer and bring to a bubble.
                          7. Add the rest of the cream, whisk again, and turn to low.
                          8. If the sauce is too thick, slowly add ¼ cup pasta water to thin it out. Set the sauce aside.
                          9. Cook the pasta about 7 minutes, then drain.
                          10. In a large bowl, mix the ricotta with the egg, herbs, remaining grated garlic clove, salt, and pepper.
                          11. In an oven-safe pan, add a thin layer of vodka sauce.
                          12. Layer: lasagna noodles, then ricotta mixture, then chicken cutlets — then repeat.
                          13. Top with mozzarella and more parmesan.
                          14. Bake at 375°F for about 40 minutes (cover if it browns too quickly).
                          15. Let sit about 10 minutes, then slice and serve.

                          Great for meal prep, freezer prep, hosting, or a friend in need. The creator's reminder: don't be cheap — good olive oil, good cheese, and good pasta make a difference.

                          Crispy Chicken Shawarma

                          Transcribed from reel caption

                          Crispy-edged shawarma chicken with lazy toum, sumac onions, and fries, wrapped and toasted until the pita is super crispy. Makes 4 sandwiches.

                          Ingredients

                            Ingredients

                              Ingredients

                                Ingredients

                                  Ingredients

                                    Steps

                                    1. Combine the chicken with the marinade ingredients; marinate for at least 30 minutes.
                                    2. Make the lazy toum; refrigerate until serving.
                                    3. Make the sumac onions; refrigerate until serving.
                                    4. Whisk the bread paste ingredients until smooth; set aside.
                                    5. Heat a large pan over very high heat and lay the chicken in a single even layer.
                                    6. Sear without flipping for about 3 minutes to build deep char and caramelized edges, then finish under the broiler for a vertical-rotisserie effect; mix and cook until done to your preferred colour.
                                    7. Build the wrap: toum, chicken, fries, pickles, sumac onions; roll it tight.
                                    8. Return the wrap to the pan and toast on all sides, pressing as you go, until golden.
                                    9. Brush the pita all over with the bread paste; toast again all over until the bread is super crispy and the paste is lightly caramelized. Serve immediately.

                                    Jalapeño Popper Chicken Cheddar Bay Biscuit Soup

                                    Transcribed from theeverydaycookbook.com (the creator's linked recipe page)

                                    This jalapeño popper chicken soup combines cream cheese, shredded cheddar, bacon, and jalapeños in a creamy chicken broth and tops individual bowls with Red Lobster Cheddar Bay biscuit dough baked until golden for an easy impressive dinner.

                                    Ingredients

                                      Ingredients

                                        Ingredients

                                          Steps

                                          1. Preheat the oven to 425°F (for the biscuit topping).
                                          2. In a Dutch oven or large pot, melt the butter over medium heat.
                                          3. Add the diced onion and diced jalapeños and cook until softened and the onions are translucent. Add the minced garlic and mix until well combined.
                                          4. Slowly add the flour in small increments, stirring constantly, until a roux forms.
                                          5. Pour in the chicken stock or bone broth and add the crushed red pepper, onion powder, garlic powder, paprika, salt, and pepper.
                                          6. Add the cooked chicken and bacon bits; stir and bring to a gentle simmer.
                                          7. After simmering 5-10 minutes, whisk in the softened cream cheese until completely smooth with no clumps.
                                          8. Reduce the heat to low and add the shredded cheddar and half and half. Stir until melted and creamy.
                                          9. Simmer for another 5-10 minutes, then taste and season with salt and pepper. (If skipping the biscuit topping, serve now garnished with bacon, cheese, green onion, or sour cream.)
                                          10. Prepare the biscuit dough according to the box directions, with the extra cheddar folded in.
                                          11. Ladle the soup into oven-safe bowls without overfilling, then gently drop 1-2 tablespoons of biscuit dough onto the top of each bowl.
                                          12. Bake uncovered for 14-16 minutes, until the dough is golden brown.
                                          13. Remove from the oven and immediately brush the hot biscuits with the garlic butter seasoning from the biscuit mix. Garnish with green onions and serve right away.

                                          Any fully cooked chicken works in place of rotisserie (baked or poached breasts shredded by hand are equally good). Instead of individual bowls, the biscuit dough can be scooped onto the soup in an oven-safe Dutch oven and baked uncovered the same way. Store soup and unbaked biscuit dough separately — the soup keeps in a sealed container in the refrigerator for up to 4 days; reheat on the stovetop over medium-low heat or in the microwave, adding a splash of broth if it thickened.

                                          Crispy Panko Chicken Tenders with Fresh Tomato Sauce

                                          Transcribed from reel

                                          The creator's go-to company dinner: crispy panko-crusted chicken tenders with a garden tomato sauce that cooks down in 30 minutes — impressive-looking and ready in half an hour.

                                          Ingredients

                                            Ingredients

                                              Steps

                                              1. Sauce: add the tomatoes, olive oil, salt, basil, garlic, onion, and a splash of water or stock to a pot.
                                              2. Cook down for about 30 minutes, until the tomato skins have broken down and their juices have released.
                                              3. Blend with an immersion blender until smooth; taste and add salt until it's just right.
                                              4. Chicken: slice each chicken breast in half, then quarter to make 4 tenders (leave larger if preferred). Pound thin between two sheets of plastic wrap.
                                              5. Coat each tender in flour, then beaten egg, then seasoned panko; repeat until all are coated.
                                              6. Heat about 1/2 inch of oil in a frying pan over medium heat; once smoking, add 2-3 cutlets and cook 3-4 minutes per side, until the panko is golden brown and the edges visibly crispy.
                                              7. Rest on a wire rack or paper towels to crisp up.
                                              8. Serve with pasta or spaghetti squash, topped with the tomato sauce, fresh grated parmesan, and chopped basil.

                                              The creator notes that sauce is 'not an exact science — measure with your heart and taste and add salt until it's just right.' The batch in the reel made far more than 2 cups of sauce because of a large tomato harvest.

                                              Butter Chicken Bunny Chow

                                              Transcribed from reel

                                              Chicken & Marinade

                                              • 4 chicken fillets, cubed
                                              • 4 thigh fillets, cubed
                                              • 6 tbsp plain yoghurt
                                              • 3 tsp ginger garlic
                                              • ½ lemon, juiced
                                              • 1½ tsp crushed cumin
                                              • 1½ tsp crushed coriander seeds
                                              • ½ tsp turmeric
                                              • 1 tsp chilli powder
                                              • 1½ tsp salt
                                              • ½ tsp black pepper

                                              Sauce

                                              • 1 tbsp ghee
                                              • 1 medium onion, finely chopped
                                              • 1½ tsp ginger garlic
                                              • 1 tsp coriander powder
                                              • 1 tsp garam masala
                                              • ½ tsp turmeric
                                              • 1 tsp chilli powder
                                              • 50 g tomato paste
                                              • 40 g butter
                                              • Cream, to finish

                                              Soft White Loaf (bunny chow bread)

                                              • 4 cups cake flour
                                              • 7 g instant yeast
                                              • 2½ tsp sugar
                                              • 1¼ tsp salt
                                              • ⅓ cup oil
                                              • ¾ cup lukewarm milk
                                              • ¾ cup lukewarm water

                                              Steps

                                              1. Add the chicken to a bowl with the yoghurt, ginger garlic, lemon juice, cumin, crushed coriander seeds, turmeric, chilli powder, salt and pepper. Mix well, cover and marinate for a few hours.
                                              2. Heat a heavy pot over medium-high heat and add the marinated chicken. Cook until nicely browned and almost cooked through. Remove and set aside.
                                              3. In the same pan, add the ghee and onion; cook until softened and lightly golden.
                                              4. Add the ginger garlic, coriander powder, garam masala, turmeric and chilli powder; cook briefly until fragrant.
                                              5. Add the tomato paste and butter; cook down a few minutes until the tomato paste darkens slightly and the butter begins to separate.
                                              6. Return the chicken to the pan and mix well so it's coated in sauce. Simmer until the chicken is cooked through and the sauce thickens.
                                              7. Finish with a generous splash of cream, stir through, taste and adjust seasoning. Top with coriander and serve.

                                              Mix, knead and proof the dough, bake into a loaf, hollow out and fill with the butter chicken.

                                              Chicken Sukka

                                              Transcribed from reel

                                              A spicy, aromatic South Indian dish with freshly ground spices and golden fried coconut. Serve with rice, roti or dosa.

                                              Steps

                                              1. To a pan, add a little oil plus cloves, cardamom, cinnamon, bay leaf, coriander seeds, cumin seeds and dry red chillies. Sauté until fragrant. Add a small tamarind piece at the end and blend everything into a smooth paste.
                                              2. In the same pan, fry freshly grated coconut until golden brown. Remove and set aside.
                                              3. Add a little more oil, then sliced onions. Sauté until softened and lightly golden.
                                              4. Add ginger-garlic paste and sauté until fragrant.
                                              5. Add the chicken, salt and turmeric. Mix well and sauté until the chicken is about 80% cooked.
                                              6. Add the ground masala paste and mix well. Cook until the masala is nicely cooked and starts releasing oil.
                                              7. Add the fried coconut and sauté everything well until the chicken is fully cooked and the flavours come together.
                                              8. Finish with a handful of fresh coriander leaves, give one last mix, and serve hot.

                                              Chicken & Chickpea Kebabs

                                              Transcribed from reel

                                              Protein-packed and healthy if air-fried.

                                              Ingredients

                                              • 2 cups soaked chickpeas (1 cup raw chickpeas soaked overnight)
                                              • 1 chicken breast
                                              • 1 potato
                                              • 2 onions
                                              • 1 tbsp coriander seeds
                                              • 1 tsp cumin seeds
                                              • 10–15 black peppercorns
                                              • 4 dried red chillies
                                              • 1 tbsp ginger-garlic paste
                                              • 1 tsp turmeric powder
                                              • 1 tsp garam masala powder
                                              • Salt
                                              • ½ cup water

                                              Steps

                                              1. In a pressure cooker add the soaked chickpeas, chicken breast, potato, onions, the whole spices, ginger-garlic paste, turmeric, garam masala, salt and ½ cup water. Cook until done.
                                              2. Open the lid and remove the chicken breast; shred it.
                                              3. For the chickpea mixture, switch on the flame and dry the mixture completely, mashing with a masher for a smooth texture. Add 1–2 tbsp ghee.
                                              4. Add the shredded chicken and mix well.
                                              5. Transfer to a bowl and let cool. Then add 1 egg, coriander leaves, 2–3 green chillies and 1 tbsp lemon juice.
                                              6. Mix well, shape into kebabs and fry in hot oil (or air-fry for a healthier version).

                                              Broccoli Cheddar Soup

                                              Transcribed from reel

                                              Panera-style, perfect for soup season.

                                              Ingredients

                                              • 4 cups broccoli florets
                                              • 1 cup shredded carrots
                                              • 1 onion, diced
                                              • 1 shallot, diced
                                              • 3 tbsp minced garlic
                                              • ½ cup unsalted butter
                                              • 2 tbsp all-purpose flour
                                              • 2 tsp smoked paprika
                                              • 1 tsp nutmeg
                                              • ½ tsp ground mustard
                                              • 2 cups chicken stock
                                              • 1 cup heavy cream
                                              • 1 cup milk (added ½ at a time)
                                              • 1 tbsp chicken bouillon
                                              • 2–3 cups extra sharp cheddar cheese, shredded
                                              • Cheddar Bay biscuits (see box directions)
                                              • Garnish: diced bacon and extra cheddar

                                              Steps

                                              Everyday Chai (makes 2 cups)

                                              Transcribed from reel

                                              A deeply spiced everyday chai for two.

                                              Ingredients

                                              • ¼ cup water
                                              • 1¾ cups milk
                                              • 5 cardamom pods, crushed
                                              • Fresh ginger, grated (lots)
                                              • 1 spoonful chai/tea masala
                                              • A few sprinkles of nutmeg
                                              • 2–3 spoonfuls loose black tea/chai
                                              • 2 heaping tbsp brown sugar
                                              • Parle-G biscuits for dipping

                                              Steps

                                              1. Add the water to a pot, then the crushed cardamom, chai masala, nutmeg and fresh ginger. Simmer together for a few minutes.
                                              2. Add the tea, then the milk. Heat until the chai starts rising toward a boil — watch it closely, it moves fast.
                                              3. Just before boiling, add the brown sugar and stir gently.
                                              4. Strain into two cups and serve hot with Parle-G.

                                              Chocolate Cream Pie with Oreo Crust

                                              https://christinabassrossetti.substack.com/p/chocolate-cream-pie

                                              Christina calls this her secret weapon for every holiday and birthday — she's been making it year-round for almost five years. An Oreo cookie crust, a rich and silky chocolate custard 'pudding' filling, and a fluffy vanilla bean whipped cream on top.

                                              Ingredients

                                                Ingredients

                                                  Ingredients

                                                    Steps

                                                    1. Heat your oven to 350 degrees.
                                                    2. Put Oreos in a food processor and pulse until the texture of sand. Add in the butter and pulse again until combined. Transfer to a pie dish and press the Oreos onto the surface of the dish until an even crust is formed. Place in the oven for 15 minutes, then let cool.
                                                    3. Whisk together the egg yolks, half & half and cream in a large bowl; set aside.
                                                    4. Add the sugar, cocoa powder, salt and corn starch to a tall-sided pan. Whisk together. Turn to medium-low heat and add in the liquid egg mixture. Whisk to combine. Stir continuously until the mixture comes to a soft boil and becomes thick — this should take about 10 minutes and be thick like a soft pudding.
                                                    5. Remove from the heat and add in the chocolate and vanilla extract. Stir until melted. Add in the butter and mix.
                                                    6. Pour the filling into the pie crust and cover with plastic wrap, letting the plastic wrap sit directly on top of the filling (this stops condensation forming in the fridge). Chill in the fridge for at least 8 hours or overnight.
                                                    7. For the whipped cream: combine the cold heavy cream, sugar and vanilla in a mixer. Beat with a whisk attachment until whipped cream forms (a couple of minutes). Serve on top of the pie, add some chocolate shavings and enjoy.
                                                    View reel

                                                        Street Style Bun Kabab

                                                        Transcribed from reel caption

                                                        Pakistani street-style bun kabab: spiced potato-and-daal kababs fried in egg, tucked into butter-toasted buns with green chutney, onion rings and ketchup.

                                                        Kabab

                                                        • 3 potatoes
                                                        • 1 cup daal channa
                                                        • 1 tablespoon freshly minced garlic
                                                        • Salt
                                                        • 1 tablespoon all spice powder
                                                        • ½ teaspoon amchoor powder
                                                        • 3–4 red round peppers
                                                        • 3–4 cloves
                                                        • 7–8 black peppercorns
                                                        • ½ teaspoon chilli flakes
                                                        • 1 teaspoon coriander powder
                                                        • 1 tablespoon pomegranate powder
                                                        • 1 tablespoon lemon juice
                                                        • 1.5 litres water
                                                        • Coriander leaves
                                                        • Chaat masala, to finish

                                                        Green Chutney

                                                        • Mint leaves
                                                        • Coriander leaves
                                                        • Green chillies
                                                        • Lemon juice
                                                        • Salt
                                                        • Roasted crushed cumin
                                                        • Yogurt

                                                        Assembly

                                                        • Buns, butter-toasted
                                                        • Whisked eggs, to coat the kababs
                                                        • Onion rings
                                                        • Ketchup

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Boil the potatoes and daal channa with all the kabab spices in 1.5 litres of water until completely cooked. (Don't use pre-boiled potatoes or daal — boiling them with the spices builds the flavour.)
                                                        2. Strain off the excess water; remove the cloves and peppercorns; mash everything with a masher or fork.
                                                        3. Mix in the lemon juice and chaat masala, let the mixture cool, then shape into kababs.
                                                        4. Dip the kababs in whisked egg and shallow-fry until golden (anda-style).
                                                        5. Blend the green chutney ingredients until smooth.
                                                        6. Toast the buns with butter and assemble with a fried kabab, green chutney, onion rings and ketchup.

                                                        Use freshly made all-spice powder and freshly grated garlic for the best flavour, and don't skip the green chutney. Soak the onion rings in iced water and drizzle with lemon before serving.

                                                        View reel

                                                        Chicken 65 with Arhar Dal

                                                        A Ramadan iftar spread: fiery, crispy Chicken 65 with curry leaves and green chillies, plus a comforting arhar (toor) dal finished with a sizzling tadka.

                                                        Chicken 65

                                                        • Bite-sized chicken pieces
                                                        • Salt, red chili flakes, crushed cumin and coriander, black pepper
                                                        • A good amount of Kashmiri red chili powder (for colour)
                                                        • A splash of soy sauce and chili sauce
                                                        • A big squeeze of ginger-garlic paste
                                                        • Some yogurt
                                                        • Cornstarch (cornflour) and rice flour, for coating
                                                        • A touch of red food colouring (optional, for restaurant-style pop)
                                                        • Fresh curry leaves and whole green chillies, for the finishing touch
                                                        • Oil, for deep frying

                                                        Arhar (Toor) Dal

                                                        • Rinsed arhar dal (split pigeon peas) and water
                                                        • Chopped onions, tomatoes, fresh coriander (cilantro), sliced green chillies
                                                        • Ginger-garlic paste, salt, red chili powder, cumin powder, coriander powder, turmeric
                                                        • For the tadka: oil or ghee, dried red chillies, cumin seeds, garlic

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Chicken 65 — marinate: season the chicken pieces with salt, red chili flakes, crushed cumin and coriander, black pepper and Kashmiri red chili powder. Add soy sauce, chili sauce, ginger-garlic paste and yogurt; mix by hand and rest a bit.
                                                        2. Chicken 65 — coat: sprinkle over cornstarch and rice flour (plus red food colouring if using) and toss until well coated.
                                                        3. Chicken 65 — fry: deep fry in hot oil until golden and crispy. Flash-fry curry leaves and whole green chillies in the same oil and toss over the chicken.
                                                        4. Dal — base: combine rinsed dal with water, onions, tomatoes, coriander, green chillies, ginger-garlic paste, salt, red chili powder, cumin, coriander powder and turmeric. Simmer until the lentils are soft and creamy; whisk or stir to break the dal down.
                                                        5. Dal — tadka: heat oil or ghee, sizzle dried red chillies, cumin seeds and garlic until fragrant, pour over the dal and cover for a minute to trap the smoky aroma.
                                                        View reel

                                                        Viral Chicken Marinade

                                                        A sweet-savoury all-purpose chicken marinade with olive oil, soy, balsamic, brown sugar and Dijon — marinate at least 4 hours before grilling.

                                                        Marinade

                                                        • 1/4 cup olive oil
                                                        • 1/4 cup soy sauce
                                                        • 1/4 cup balsamic vinegar
                                                        • 1/4 cup brown sugar
                                                        • 2 tbsp Dijon mustard

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Whisk all marinade ingredients together and coat the chicken.

                                                        Marry Me Paneer Bakes

                                                        The viral Marry Me sauce — creamy, garlicky, sundried-tomato — with paneer instead of chicken, baked in golden puff pastry squares. Makes 7–8.

                                                        Filling

                                                        • 250g paneer, cut into small cubes
                                                        • 3 tbsp oil
                                                        • 3 garlic cloves, finely chopped
                                                        • 1 tbsp plain flour
                                                        • 350ml vegetable stock
                                                        • 1 tsp onion powder
                                                        • 1 tsp paprika
                                                        • 1 tsp oregano
                                                        • 1/2 tsp black pepper
                                                        • 1/2 tsp salt
                                                        • 2 tsp sundried tomato paste
                                                        • 2–3 tbsp chopped sundried tomatoes
                                                        • 100g baby spinach
                                                        • 100ml double cream
                                                        • Puff pastry
                                                        • Egg or milk, for brushing

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Heat the oil in a pan and fry the paneer cubes for 3–4 minutes, turning occasionally, until golden on all sides. Transfer to a bowl of hot water and set aside to keep soft.
                                                        2. In the same pan, cook the garlic for 1 minute until fragrant. Stir in the flour and cook for 2 minutes, stirring constantly.
                                                        3. Gradually pour in the vegetable stock, stirring well to make a smooth sauce. Add the seasonings, sundried tomato paste and chopped sundried tomatoes; simmer 2–3 minutes until slightly thickened.
                                                        4. Add the baby spinach and cook 2–3 minutes until wilted.
                                                        5. Drain the paneer and add to the sauce with the double cream. Simmer gently 2–3 minutes until creamy, then remove from the heat and cool slightly.
                                                        6. Cut puff pastry into squares, spoon about 2 tbsp filling into the centre of each, fold over and seal the edges well with a fork.
                                                        7. Brush with egg or milk and bake at 200°C (180°C fan) for 20–25 minutes, until puffed and golden.

                                                        Chicken Potstickers

                                                        https://nombeah.com/the-best-chicken-potstickers/

                                                        Juicy chicken potstickers with a flavourful sticky filling — pan-fried until crisp on the bottom, steamed through, and perfect for freezing.

                                                        Filling

                                                        • 500g chicken thighs (or minced chicken)
                                                        • 1 package thin dumpling wrappers
                                                        • 1/4 yellow onion, finely chopped
                                                        • 1 tbsp ginger, minced
                                                        • 5–6 garlic cloves, minced
                                                        • 3 green onions, chopped
                                                        • Cilantro
                                                        • 1 tsp cornstarch
                                                        • 3 tbsp cold water
                                                        • 1 1/2 tbsp soy sauce
                                                        • 1 tbsp oyster sauce
                                                        • 1/4 tsp cumin powder
                                                        • 1/2–3/4 tsp salt
                                                        • 1/4 tsp ground black pepper
                                                        • 1/8 tsp MSG

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Pulse the chicken thighs in a food processor until fairly smooth; transfer to a bowl.
                                                        2. Mix the cornstarch and water into a slurry and add to the chicken with the onion, garlic, ginger, scallions, cilantro, soy sauce, oyster sauce, salt, MSG and cumin.
                                                        3. Mix in one direction with a spoon or chopsticks until sticky and uniform.
                                                        4. Taste-test: microwave a teaspoon of filling 10–15 seconds, taste and adjust seasoning.
                                                        5. Place about a tablespoon of filling in the centre of a wrapper, wet the edges of one side with water, pinch closed in the centre and pleat inwards to the end. Repeat.
                                                        6. To freeze: place on a tray, freeze 30 minutes to an hour, then transfer to a freezer-safe bag (up to 3 months).
                                                        7. To cook: heat a pan with oil over medium-high heat, place dumplings flat-side down; once the bottom is lightly golden, pour in enough hot water to cover the pan bottom, lid on, reduce to medium-low and cook until the water evaporates and the potstickers look shiny.
                                                        8. Remove the lid, turn the heat up and crisp the bottoms. Serve with your favourite dipping sauce.

                                                        Use chicken thighs for juiciness; if using lean ground chicken, mix in 1–2 tbsp oil or butter.

                                                        View reel

                                                        Three Chicken Marinades for the Week

                                                        Three meal-prep chicken marinades — tandoori, jalapeño-cilantro and chipotle-cilantro — each built for 2 lbs of chicken thighs.

                                                        Tandoori Chicken Marinade

                                                        • 2 lbs chicken thighs, cubed
                                                        • 4 tbsp olive oil
                                                        • 1/2 cup yogurt
                                                        • 2 tsp fresh ginger and garlic paste
                                                        • 2 tsp garam masala
                                                        • 2 tsp salt
                                                        • 2 tsp cumin powder
                                                        • 2 tsp chili powder
                                                        • 1/2 tbsp turmeric
                                                        • 1 tsp coriander powder
                                                        • 1 tsp paprika (optional)
                                                        • Juice of 1 whole lime

                                                        Jalapeño Cilantro Marinade

                                                        • 2 lbs chicken thighs, chopped
                                                        • 2 bunches cilantro
                                                        • 3 whole jalapeños
                                                        • 2 tsp salt
                                                        • 1 tsp smoked paprika
                                                        • 2 tsp cumin powder
                                                        • 1/2 tsp black pepper powder
                                                        • 7 garlic cloves
                                                        • 1/2 cup mayonnaise
                                                        • 1/2 cup yogurt
                                                        • Juice of 1 whole lime

                                                        Chipotle Cilantro Marinade

                                                        • 2 lbs chicken thighs, cubed
                                                        • 1/2 medium red onion
                                                        • 4 garlic cloves
                                                        • 4 tbsp canned chipotle peppers in adobo sauce
                                                        • 3 tbsp olive oil
                                                        • 3 tbsp ancho chile powder
                                                        • 2 tsp cumin powder
                                                        • 1 1/2 tbsp salt
                                                        • 1 tsp black pepper powder
                                                        • Splash of water

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Tandoori: combine the cubed thighs with all marinade ingredients; mix well.
                                                        2. Jalapeño cilantro: blend the cilantro, jalapeños, salt, smoked paprika, cumin, black pepper, garlic, mayonnaise, yogurt and lime juice into a paste; mix through the chopped thighs.
                                                        3. Chipotle cilantro: blend the red onion, garlic, chipotle peppers in adobo, olive oil, ancho chile powder, cumin, salt, black pepper and a splash of water into a paste; mix through the cubed thighs.
                                                        4. Marinate, then cook as desired — ideal for a week of flavour.

                                                        Multivitamin Chutney

                                                        A dry multivitamin chutney powder — moringa and curry leaves with flaxseeds, sesame, roasted chana and peanuts, sweet-soured with jaggery and tamarind. Keeps 1–1.5 months.

                                                        Dry Ingredients

                                                        • 3–4 cups moringa leaves (fresh or dried, or moringa powder)
                                                        • 2 cups curry leaves (fresh or dried)
                                                        • 2 tbsp flaxseeds
                                                        • 2 tbsp sesame seeds
                                                        • 1 cup roasted chana
                                                        • 1/4 cup roasted peanuts

                                                        Flavouring & Seasoning

                                                        • 2 tbsp jaggery (grated or powdered)
                                                        • 1 tbsp tamarind (pulp or a small piece of seedless tamarind)
                                                        • Black salt, to taste
                                                        • 2 tbsp red chili powder (adjust to taste)
                                                        • 1/2 tsp turmeric powder

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Wash and dry the moringa and curry leaves thoroughly. On a low flame, dry-roast the leaves until all moisture evaporates (or use an oven/air fryer, or moringa powder).
                                                        2. In a dry pan, roast the flaxseeds and sesame seeds on medium heat until golden and aromatic; cool.
                                                        3. In a mixer jar, grind the roasted chana, peanuts, flaxseeds, sesame seeds, jaggery, tamarind, black salt, red chili powder and turmeric into a coarse powder.
                                                        4. Grind the dried moringa and curry leaves into a fine powder (same jar or separately) and mix into the chutney base until well combined.
                                                        5. Store in an airtight container in a cool, dry place; stays fresh 1–1.5 months.

                                                        Cheesy Truffle Hashbrowns

                                                        Crispy air-fried hashbrown patties loaded with mozzarella, garlic aioli, parmesan and a drizzle of truffle oil — a fancy-feeling snack or girl-dinner appetizer.

                                                        S'more Espresso Cocktail

                                                        A dessert cocktail: chilled espresso shaken with chocolate graham cracker milk, charred marshmallow syrup, bitters and egg white, crowned with toasted marshmallow.

                                                        Cocktail

                                                        • 2 oz chocolate graham cracker milk (below)
                                                        • 2 oz chilled espresso
                                                        • 1/2 oz charred marshmallow syrup (below)
                                                        • 2 heavy dashes spiced bitters
                                                        • Pinch of salt
                                                        • 1 egg white
                                                        • Toasted marshmallow, for garnish

                                                        Charred Marshmallow Syrup

                                                        • 50g (8) jumbo marshmallows
                                                        • 200g demerara sugar
                                                        • 200g water

                                                        Chocolate Graham Cracker Milk

                                                        • 50g graham crackers
                                                        • 250g chocolate milk

                                                        Steps

                                                        1. Chill the espresso (pour into a Hyperchiller or similar for 30s).
                                                        2. Add the milk, chilled espresso, syrup, bitters, salt and egg white to a shaker; dry-shake or hand-froth 15–20 seconds until emulsified and frothy.
                                                        3. Strain into a coupe and garnish with speared, toasted marshmallows.
                                                        4. Optional: chill the coupe in the freezer 10 minutes, smoke it over smouldering chips for 45+ seconds, then add the drink.
                                                        5. Charred marshmallow syrup: torch the tops of the marshmallows until charred; set aside. Simmer the sugar and water in a small saucepan until combined, add the marshmallows on low heat and stir, breaking them up, until fully dissolved (pull off the heat if it froths). Cool, strain through a fine sieve. Keeps 2 weeks refrigerated.
                                                        6. Chocolate graham cracker milk: break up the graham crackers into the chocolate milk, steep covered in the fridge 30 minutes, strain. Keeps 5 days refrigerated.
